Subject:
Change to Scope of Work for 25th and Carey Intersection
Dear Ms. Gerard:
Western Research & Development, Ud. (Western) has been providing design services for the
signalized intersection at 2S St and Carey Ave.
During the signal design, Western noticed the
asphalt conditions on 25th Street and the ADA ramps were in very poor condition. Western and City
of Cheyenne staff met on site on August 28, 2019 to inspect and discuss this issue.
Both parties
discussed the extent of road reconstruction, curb and gutter replacement, ADA ramp replacement,
and storm sewer inlet replacements required for the project.
Western’s understanding of the
additional scope of work of the project is as follows:
•
Reconstruction of 25th Street from the west side of Carey Avenue to the new access on
(west side of 2504 Carey Avenue property). Transition from existing to new asphalt will use a
wedge design that eases the impact on vehicles traversing the area. This area encompasses
approximately 6,000 square feet of asphalt road replacement (150 lineal feet of 40-foot-wide
road).
•
Curb and gutter will be replaced on the north side of 25’ in the same area as the road
reconstruction.
Curb and gutter replacement will continue north on Carey Ave to the
northeast corner of 2504 Carey Avenue.
This is approximately 200 lineal feet of curb and
gutter replacement.
•
Along with the curb and gutter replacement, the existing storm inlets on the northwest corner
of the Carey and 25 intersection are to be replaced (2 inlets).
•
ADA ramps on the west side of the intersection are to be designed.
These ramps will be
coordinated with the sidewalk and ramp construction on the east side of the intersection (part
of the Capital Reconstruction project).
A geotechnical investigation is not included in the proposed scope.
Western proposes that the
subgrade be reworked, covered with 6” of Grade W base, and a minimum of 3.5” asphalt concrete for
the road section. Western also recommends incorporating fly ash into the subgrade preparation.
This is a small additional construction cost that significantly adds to the structural stability of the road
reconstruction.

Western proposes a fee of $9,950.00 for the design and bid assistance on the increased scope of
work. This fee includes the development of construction documents, responding to City comments,
providing supplemental specifications, a final bid tab, and cost estimate. Western will also attend the
pre-bid meeting and respond to contractor questions.
At the City’s option, Western could also provide construction inspection for a fee of $5,000.00. This
fee would include a weekly site visit, corresponding weekly report, review of submittals from
contractor, respond to requests for information from the contractor, and regular meetings with the
City to keep staff updated on the status of the project.
If the City would like some construction
inspection assistance, but not the entire scope proposed, Western will be happy to negotiate the
construction inspection fee.
We appreciate the opportunity to work on this expanded project with you. Please contact me
regarding any questions or concerns with this proposal.
Sincerely,

The above referenced contract is amended or modified as set forth in the following and these
amendments and modifications ofthe plans and specifications are agreed to by the parties. All
other aspects ofthe original agreement remain unchanged and in full force and effect.
I. Description of Changes
Contract was procured and written to enable the City to add additional tasks to the original
contract via contract modification. This is the first additional task to be added. This additional
task is to provide design services for a new traffic signal to be installed at the intersection of
Storey Blvd. & Ridge Rd.
II. New Contract Items
The City is planning to install a new traffic signal at the intersection of Storey Blvd. & Ridge Rd.
Traffic signal and intersection design is to be completed in the spring of2OlS. Scope of
engineering services for the traffic signal analysis and design shall include traffic data collection,
capacity and queuing analysis, crash analysis, developing optimized traffic signal timing and
coordination plans, identification ofROW needs, and traffic signalization design and
construction drawings. See attached memo for breakdown ofdesign costs.
UI. Schedule
The design work is anticipated to take approximately ten weeks from when this modification is
executed. The entire contract will terminate when all items involved have been completed. The
original procurement stated that no new tasks would be added to this contract after June 30,
2018.

All work (preliminary, draft, and final) performed by the Firm tinder this
Agreement is the property of the City. The City will own any and all data, documents,
workingpapers, computer programs, photographs, and other material produced by the Finn
pursuant to this Agreement, and the Fizm hereby assigns and transfers to the City any and
all intellectual property rights for such materials. The Firm will provide the City with
copies of all such materials. Under no circumstances, including pending disputes between
the City and the Firm, will the Firm fail to deliver possession of said documents and
materials to the City upon demand.
21.
CONFLICT 01? INTEREST:
In entering this Agreement, the Firm represents and warrants that it has not conducted any
work that may constitute a conflict of interest that would render any of its services under this
Agreement non-reimbursable under this Agreement or any federal regulations that may be
incorporated into said contract.
The Firm further represents and warrants it presently has no
interest, and shall not acquire any interest, direct or indirect, financial or otherwise, which would
conflict in any manner or degree with performance ofthe services hereunder. In addition, the Firm
covenants that in the performance of this contract, no subcontractor or person having such an
interest shall be employed. The Firm certifies that no one who has or will have any financial
interest under this contract is an officer or employee ofthe City.
22.
ACCEPTANCE NOT WAIVER:
The City’s approval of drawings, plans, specifications, reports, and incidental work or
materials furnished hereunder shall not in any way relieve the Firm from responsibility for the
technical accuracy ofthe work. The City’s approval or acceptance of; or payment for, any services
shall not be construed to operate as a waiver of any of the City’s rights under this Agreement or
any of its legal rights under statute and common law arising out of the performance of this
Agreement.
23.
DEFAULT:
Each and every term and condition herein shall be deemed a material element of the
Agreement. In the event either party shall fail or refuse to perform according to the terms ofthis
Agreement such party may be declared in default.
24.
REMEDIES:
In the event a party declares the other party in default hereof, said party declaring default
shall notify the defaulting party in writing and such defaulting party shall be allowed a period of
fifteen (15) days to cure said default. In the event that the default remains uncorrected, the party
not in default may elect to: (a) terminate the Agreement and seek damages; (b) treat the Agreement
as continuing and require specific performance; or (c) avail itself of any other remedy at law or
equity. In the event the Firm fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the City

may elect to make good such deficiencies and charge the Firm therefore.
25.
TERMINATION:
The City may, by written notice to Firm, terminate this Agreement in whole or in part by
giving Firm five(S) calendar days’ written notice. The effective date oftermination shall be five
(5) calendar days from the date of the notice of termination.
Upon receipt of such notice of
tennination, the Firm shall:
a.
Discontinue all services affected by the notice (unless the notice directs
otherwise); and
b.
Deliver to the City, within five (5) calendar days from the date ofthe notice
of termination, all data, designs, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries
and all other work that has been performed or is to be performed under this Agreement,
whether completed or in process. In the event of temiination, the City will pay the Firm
for all labor and services supplied and accepted as ofthe effective date oftermination.
26.
WAIVER:
The waiver by either party of any term, condition or covenant, or breach of any term,
condition or covenant, shall not constitute a waiver of any other term, condition or covenant, or
breach thereof.
27.
SEVERABILITY:
If any provision, section, subsection, sentence, clause, or phrase of this Agreement is
invalidated by any court ofcompetent jurisdiction, such holding shall not affect the validity ofthe
remainder ofthis Agreement, which shall continue in full force and effect.
28.
SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS:
All the terms, conditions, and provisions herein shall inure to the benefit of and be binding
upon the parties hereto and their respective permitted successors and assigns.
29.
ASSIGNMENT:
Neither party shall assign this Agreement without prior written consent of both parties.
Any delegation or assignment shall not operate to relieve either party of its responsibilities
hereunder. However, nothing in this Agreement shall prohibit the Firm from subcontracting to a
qualified subcontractor.
30.
CONFIDENTiALITY OF INFORMATION:
If applicable, notwithstanding the release of records as required by the Wyoming Public
Records Act, Wyo. Stat. § 16-4-201, et seq., all documents, data compilations, reports, computer

programs, photographs and any other work provided to or produced by the Firm exclusive to the
performance ofthis Agreement, shall be kept confidential by any Firm unless written permission

31.
ETHICS:
The Firm shall keep informed ofand comply with the Wyoming Ethics and Disclosure Act
(Wyo. Stat. § 9-13-101, etseq.,) and any and all ethical standards governing the City and the Firm.
32.
FORCE MAJEURE:
Neither party shall be liable for failure to perform under this Agreement if such failure to
perform arises out of cases beyond the control and without fault or negligence of the
nonperforming party. Such causes may include, but are not limited to, acts of God or the public
enemy, fires, floods, epidemics, quarantine restrictions, freight embargoes, and unusually severe
weather. This provision shall become effective only if the party failing to perform immediately
notifies the other party ofthe extent and nature ofthe problem, limits delay in performance to that
required by the event, and takes all reasonable steps to minimize delays. This provision shall not
be effective unless the failure to perform is beyond the control and without the fault or negligence
ofthe nonperforming party.
33.
THIRD-PARTY RIGHTS:
The Parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity the status ofthird party
beneficiary, and this Agreement shall not be construed so as to create such status. The rights,
duties and obligations contained in this Agreement shall operate only between the parties to this
Agreement, and shall inure solely to the benefit of the parties to this Agreement. The parties to

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
EXHIBIT “A”
SCOPE OF WORK
A. Project Description
Purpose: The City ofCheyenne is hereby soliciting consultant proposals for professional
services for Fiscal Years 2017-2018 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES. This
request invites qualified consultants to submit proposals for accomplishments of the items of
work described below under Scope of Services. Proposals shall be prepared and submitted in
accordance with the requirements described in this Request for Proposals (RFP). The City is
interested in selecting a consulting firm to provide on-call City-wide traffic engineering services,
as needed. Once the firms are selected, task orders will be negotiated for individual projects, as
projects are identified for approval by the Governing Body. Specific traffic engineering tasks for
identified projects for this RFP will be defined by an individual scope of work and cost estimate
prepared for each specific task/project. The cost estimate for each specific task/project will be
based on an hourly not-to-exceed fee amount. On-call professional services selection will be
effective for a period of approximately 18 months, through June 2018.
There is no specific allocated dollar amount dedicated or assigned to anticipated tasks or projects
to be accomplished. The City does not guarantee a set amount ofwork or agreement value for
potential services.
General: The proposed Scope of Services is not intended to be a detailed scope of work that
wifi be required as a part of the fmal professional services agreement(s), but is intended to
provide general information to firms wishing to submit proposals. The City will negotiate
the detailed Scope of Services with the successful firms as individual tasks/projects are
identified, should the City elect to proceed with the projects.
In general, the City will work with the selected firms on a project by project basis to determine
the types of services required on a project. The consultants are expected to be able to provide on-
call traffic engineering services as required. The services performed by the consulting firms may
include, but are not limited to:
Li Existing Traffic Data Collection and Review.
Li Traffic Counts.
Li Traffic Forecasting.
Li Traffic Impact Study.
LI Capacity Analysis.
C Crash Analysis.
LI Traffic Operations Analysis.
Li Traffic Simulation.
A-i

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
El Traffic Signal Analysis, Warrant Study, and Left Turn Phasing Study.
LI Stop Sign Warrant Analysis.
El School Traffic Issues Observation and Study.
El Traffic Control Plans.
El Speed Study.
LI Sight Distance Analysis.
El Access Management Analysis and Study.
El Bicycle and Pedestrian Facility Planning and Design.
Li Traffic Signal Optimization.
U Traffic Signal Design and Plans.
U Isolated intersection improvements
El Miscellaneous Traffic Engineering Assistance.
The following projects are authorized for the first round of On-Call Traffic Engineering
Services:
Deming Dr. & W.
St. Signal Design
The City is planning to install a new traffic signal at the intersection of Deming Dr. & W. 5th s•
Traffic signal and intersection design is to be completed in the spring of 2017. The existing
bridge over Crow Creek will be a design constraint as widening or replacement of the structure is
not within the scope of this project. Scope of engineering services for the traffic signal analysis
and design may include traffic data collection, capacity and queuing analysis, crash analysis,
developing optimized traffic signal timing and coordination plans, identification of ROW needs,
and traffic signalization design and construction drawings.
Yellowstone Rd. & Vandehei Ave. Signal Modifications
When the City added left turn phasing for eastbound and westbound traffic to the intersection of
Yellowstone Rd. & Vandehei Ave. in 2011, the load rating ofthe existing poles/mastarms was
not verified. Subsequently, it was determined that the eastbound and westbound poles/mastarms
are not rated for left turn heads and need to be replaced with poles/mastarms meeting WYDOT
Load Case 2. Scope of engineering services for the signal modification design may include
identification ofROW needs and traffic signalization design and construction drawings.
Walterscheid Blvd & W. Fox Farm Rd. Signal Design
The City is planning to install a new traffic signal at the intersection ofWalterscheid Blvd. & W.
Fox Farm Rd. In conjunction with the installation of a traffic signal, some roadway geometric
improvements will be required, especially on W. Fox Farm Rd., to better align the through lanes
and provide dedicated left turn lanes. Scope of engineering services for the traffic signal analysis
and design may include traffic data collection, capacity and queuing analysis, crash analysis,
A-2

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
developing optimized traffic signal timing and coordination plans, identification of ROW needs,
and traffic signalization design and construction drawings.
DESIGN OF TRAFFIC ENGINEERING PLANS
This work is for design oftraffic signals, roadway intersection modification, signs, pavement
markings/markers, lighting, other operational safety improvements and transportation
justification studies and warrant analyses. Existing or proposed plans will be provided if
available. If, however, no plans are available, field investigation, survey, right ofway research
and verification of existing topography, utilities, infrastructure, and traffic control equipment
shall be conducted. This investigation shall include, but shall not be limited to, such items as
utilities, utility potholing, drainage, ADA accessibility, pedestrian safety and circulation, lane
usage, potential conflicts with a proposed design, coordination and review by other agencies,
City review and permitting, and all information necessary to implement the proposed
improvements. The Consultant shall be the point of contact and make all submittals to other
agencies on behalf ofthe City.
All proposals must be made on the basis of, and either meet or exceed, the requirements
contained herein. These improvements will be approved by the City of Cheyenne and installed
in the City’s right-of—way or easements. All individuals performing work under this Contract
must have the appropriate licenses, certifications or credentials that prove competence in tasks
being performed.
Offerors should provide examples ofthree (3) traffic signal and intersection improvement
modification design projects that have been completed within the past five (5) years. Each
project example shall include the following information: a detailed description ofthe project
scope of work, including project duration, completion date, design and construction costs,
concerns encountered and there solutions, and any relevant features, to include photos. Include
the names and contact information for project clients, project managers and designers. These
qualifying projects must be contracted to or have been completed by the offeror, not
subconsultants.
1. DESIGN AND CONSTRUCTION STANDARDS
All design and construction shall be done in accordance with the latest edition ofthe
applicable standards including, but not limited to, the following:
•
AASHTO Policy on Geometric Design ofHighways and Streets
•
Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices
•
Americans with Disabilities Act Accessibility Guidelines. U.S. Department of Justice,
United States Access Board
•
City of Cheyenne and Board of Public Utilities Construction Standards &
Specifications
•
City of Cheyenne Drainage Standards
A-3

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
Conflicts between design and construction standards shall be resolved by the City.
2.
SERVICES
The consultant will work closely with the City of Cheyenne Project Manager to perform the
work necessary to create the final plan, specifications and estimate package along with
supporting engineering and environmental documents for this project based on standard
WYDOT and City formats. Detailed scopes ofwork will be developed for each individual
project at the time the successful consultant is authorized to proceed.
B. General Conditions
The following items are considered to be included as part of the basic compensation for this
project:
•
Normal business expenses - phone, postage, etc.
•
Cost of insurance
•
In-house computer time and service
•
Word processing, accounting, and man-hour records
•
Permits and license fees
•
Mileage
•
Travel fees, room and board, per diem
•
Printing costs for all standard review, bidding, and as-constructed plans and other
correspondence and contract documents
•
Film and processing
•
Overtime engineering and inspection
•
Survey materials, stakes, etc
•
Additional required services
Any changes in the Consultant’s or Subconsultant’s staff or fee structure shall be presented in
writing to the City for approval prior to initiating any changes or performing any work.
C. Duties. Responsibilities of the Consultant
The Consultant shall supervise and be accountable for all engineering matters related to the
project. This responsibility shall extend to supervision and responsibility for the actions of
subcontractors or subconsultants working for the Consultant.
CONSULTANT’S ENGINEER WILL:
1.
Contractor Selection:
Advise City’s Representative regarding recommendation for the selection of a Contractor
and any subcontractors.

Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
2.
Schedules:
Review the progress schedule, schedule of Shop Drawing submissions and schedule of
values prepared by Contractor and consult with City’s Representative concerning their
acceptability.
3.
Conferences:
Conduct and keep minutes of pre-bid and pre-construction conferences with contractors.
4.
Liaison:
Serve as the City’s liaison with Contractor to implement the intent of the Contract
Documents.
5.
Shop Drawings and Samples:
a.
Receive samples and shop drawing furnished by contractor, record date of their
receipt, and track progress of approvals. Advise City’s Representative of their
acceptability.
b.
Verify that tests, equipment and systems startup, operation and maintenance
instructions are conducted as required by the Contract documents, and that
Contractor maintains adequate records thereof observe, record and report to the
City’s Representative appropriate details relative to the test procedures and
startups.
7.
Interpretation of Contract Documents:
Transmit to Contractor the City’s clarifications and interpretations ofthe Contract
Documents.
8.
Modifications:
Consider and evaluate any changes or modifications in Drawings or Specifications and
report them with recommendations to the City Representative for approval prior to
authorizing any such changes or charges to Force Account or other items. Administer
Change Orders, Contract Modifications and extra Payment Requests.
9.
Records:
a.
Maintain files for correspondence, reports ofjob conferences, Shop Drawings and
samples submissions, reproductions of original contract Documents including all
addenda, change orders, field orders, additional Drawings issued subsequent to
the execution ofthe Contract, City’s Representative clarifications and

Record names, addresses and telephone numbers of all Contractors,

condition of such.

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
10. Reports:
a.
Furnish the City’s Representative weekly summary reports including the approved
progress schedule, and schedule of Shop Drawing submissions.
b.
Consult the City’s Representative in advance of scheduled start ofimportant
phases of the Work.
11. Payment Requisitions:
Review and certify applications using forms provided by the City for compliance with the
established procedure for their submission and forward them with recommendations to
the City’s Representative noting particularly their relation to the schedule of values, work
completed and materials and equipment delivered or paid for at the site, but not
incorporated in the Work.
12. Certificates, Maintenance and Operation Manuals:
During the course ofthe Work, verify that contractor has supplied all certificates,
maintenance and operation manuals and other data needed to operate machinery and
equipment actually installed and collect all such materials for presentation to City with
final construction report.
13. Completion:
a.
Submit to Contractor a punch list of observed items requiring completion or
correction.
b.
Conduct final inspection in the company ofthe City’s Representative, Contractor
and utility companies involved and prepare a final list of items to be completed or
corrected.
c.
Verify that all items on the final list have been completed or corrected, make
recommendations to the City concerning acceptance and prepare a certificate of
Substantial Completion.
d.
Conduct eleventh (11th) and twenty-third (23rd) month inspections with the City
Representative and Contractor’s Representatives and report findings to the City’s
Representative immediately prior to the expiration of the two year or other
warranty, as applicable.
e.
After completion ofthe project prepare and submit to City certified “Record
Drawing” pians showing final accepted conditions ofthe project. The record
drawings shall consist of one set of reproducible mylars, one set ofprints and an
electronic copy in AutoCAD 2014 through current version DXF or DWG format
OR Land desktop/Civil 3D Design. The record drawings shall be submitted to the
City within 90 days ofproject completion. A certified final construction report of
conditions, maintenance items, inspection reports, contract modifications,
correspondence, field orders and any other pertinent information shall be
furnished by the project engineer to the City.
A-6

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT
Project Name: FY17-18 ON-CALL TRAFFIC ENGINEERING SERVICES
f.
Assist the City staffto implement the final quantities and payments ofthis project
to the City’s computerized job accumulation program.
D. Limitations of Authority of the Consultant
Except upon written instructions of City’s Representative, Consultant:
1.
Shall not authorize any deviation from the Contract Documents or costs, or approve any
substitute materials or equipment, without consulting the Owner.
2.
Shall not exceed limitations on the City’s authority as set forth in the Contract
Documents.
3.
Shall not undertake any ofthe responsibilities of the Contractor, subcontractors, utility
companies or responsibilities oftheir superintendent, to expedite the work.
4.
Shall not advise on or issue directions relative to any aspect ofthe means, methods,
techniques, sequences or procedures of construction unless such is specifically called for
in the Contract Documents.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-07-01_TotalExtraction_60147126.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 12, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Ken Esquibel (via Zoom), 
Bryan Cook (arriving at 6:12 p.m.), Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White (via Zoom).  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The 
pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda).  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Segrave and Dr. Rinne, agenda item #22 was removed from the Consent Agenda. 
Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 28, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive 
(Postponed from June 28, 2021).  Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, advised the 
annexation is dependent upon a county lot line adjustment which was denied and failed during an 
appeal.  The applicant is seeking reconsideration by the County Commissioners on July 20, 2021 
and staff and the applicant agree that postponing the item for 2 weeks would allow reconsideration 
instead of postponing indefinitely.  Mr. Lloyd noted this is affiliated with the next item on the 
agenda which is for the zone change.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved 
to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone 
indefinitely, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted that city staff and the applicant have requested 
the motion to postpone indefinitely be voted down and instead postpone to the next meeting of the 
governing body.  Motion failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body present.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to postpone to July 26, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County AR Agricultural Residential to MR 
Medium-Density Residential for land located southwest of the intersection of Braehill Road, 
Parkhill Road, and Panorama Drive (Postponed from June 28, 2021).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone indefinitely, 

   
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mayor Collins noted the desire of the staff and applicant is to vote 
down the motion to postpone indefinitely and instead postpone to the next meeting of the 
Governing Body on July 26, 2021.  Motion failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing 
body present.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to July 26, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion 
to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road. 
Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department, explained the staff report indicated inclusion 
of the Dell Range right-of-way, however, there is consideration of including the right-of-way at 
the location due to WYDOT improvements. City staff including the City Engineer have confirmed 
the interest to include the right-of-way in the annexation and staff has requested a 2-week 
postponement to allow for a map revision to include the right-of-way in the ordinance and affiliated 
map. Mr. Lloyd advised the postponement isn’t anticipated to delay the project tremendously as 
the final plat will still have to be submitted.  (It was noted for the record Bryan Cook, Councilman 
Ward II, joined the meeting at 6:12 during discussion and prior to vote on this item). Vicki Million-
Hughes provided a handout related to the annexation and project proposal for the land noting the 
developer would like to place 50+ homes on the land.  She commented on many concerns including 
obstructed views of the city skyline therefore requesting ranch style homes and buffers between 
properties, concern of what commercial property may be located on the land, accidents and traffic 
in the area and lack of green space in the subdivision.  It was noted the ordinance is to annex the 
land into the city and the related ordinance will create the zoning. It was advised that the final plat 
for development actions will be submitted at a later date.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to July 26, 2021, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Lloyd explained upon inquiry that this project contained an 
agreement written into the original documents from county residential property owners on the 
hillside adjacent to the area that the property down the hillside would be serviced by city sewer 
services and it would be anticipated to be developed for urban uses in the future.   Motion to 
postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING –  Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU Mixed Use and County LR Low 
Density Residential to CB Community Business, LR Low-Density Residential, and MR Medium-
Density Residential for land located northeast of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range 
Boulevard, west of Whitney Road.  Patty Lockley requested a meeting with the developer and 
nearby residents in the area be held prior to the next City Council meeting.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to 
July 26, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion to postpone carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 8.60.210, Liens, of Chapter 8.60, 
Nuisances, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mayor 
Collins explained he had met with members of the banking community and the Chamber of 
Commerce and the recommendation at this time is that the ordinance and the 2 following affiliated 
ordinances be postponed indefinitely.  Mayor Collins reported the effected parties will work on a 

   
solution for the lien situation to be brought forward in the near future.  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from the Committee.  It was noted 
a main motion is necessary prior to a motion to postpone.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave.  City Attorney Michael O’Donnell, upon inquiry, provided procedural information 
relating to the motion to postpone indefinitely explaining it essentially kills an item, however, 
allows for the item to be reintroduced at a later time. Dale Steenbergen, President/CEO for the 
Chamber of Commerce, confirmed his willingness to serve on a committee to find a compromise 
to the lien issue between the City and the financial community.  Concern was voiced relating to 
the motion to postpone indefinitely and it was noted that particular motion hasn’t been common 
practice for the governing body.  Motion to postpone indefinitely carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 12.04.240, Liens, of Chapter 
12.04, Streets, Sidewalks, and Driveways Generally, of Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public 
Places, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal advised there was no 
recommendation from the Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  
Motion to postpone indefinitely carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating a new Section 15.24.030, Liens, of Chapter 
15.24, Dangerous Building Code, of Title 15, Buildings and Construction, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal advised there was no recommendation from the Committee.  
Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to postpone indefinitely, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion to postpone indefinitely carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson and Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.04, Business 
Licenses Generally, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4384) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.52, Massage 
Establishments, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4385) 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 8.60, Nuisances, 
of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal advised 
there was no recommendation from the Committee.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. O’Donnell explained the amendment brings the 
ordinance up to date with the new administrative hearing process. Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell 
explained that due to a miscommunication, this item did not receive a recommendation at the July 

   
6, 2021 Finance Committee meeting.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. (#4386) 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, a portion 
of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, Laramie County, 
Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted the annexation is unique due 
to the leased property being located adjacent to Warren Air Force Base (WAFB) and a request was 
made for additional information on the process.  Comments were made regarding lack of WAFB 
involvement in the annexation process, history of previous land uses at this location, current grant 
opportunities relating to utility development on the site and information was provided regarding 
options for annexation of the land.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County X (Exempt) to SS (Support Services) 
for a portion of the S½ of Section 35, and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W, 
Laramie County, Wyoming generally located southwest of Interstate 25 and Happy Jack Road. Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential-Medium Density for land located northwest of the intersection of 
Country Club Avenue and Snyder Avenue.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
create a Public Safety Fee requirement for residential, commercial, service, industrial, civic, public 
service, employment, agriculture, and other development in the city.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members-Term-Vacancies, 
of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of 
the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave. Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute dated July 6, 2021, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave. Mr. O’Donnell explained the substitute provides for initial Downtown 
Development Authority Board appointment to be made by the Mayor and adds a process to send 
suggestions to the Mayor for consideration.  Support was expressed for the process and it was 
noted this is needed to help achieve goals for the downtown district.  Motion to amend by substitute 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and 
Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Mr. Johnson voting “no”.   

   
 
RESOLUTION – Approving propositions to impose the 1% Specific Purpose Sales and 
Use Tax in Laramie County, State of Wyoming, for the exclusive purpose of funding specific 
purposes for specific amounts; the propositions to be voted on at a special election to be held 
November 2, 2021.  Gunnar Malm, Laramie County Commissioner, explained that as of tonight, 
Laramie County and the towns of Albin, Pine Bluffs and Burns will have approved their resolutions 
and noted he felt the process went well, expressing appreciation to city staff.  Mayor Collins 
thanked Laramie County Commissioners for their assistance during the process.  Mr. Roybal 
moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated July 
6, 2021 at 1:52 p.m. which includes the following amendments made at the July 6, 2021 Finance 
Committee meeting: clarification to proposition numbering and removal of the “17th Street 
Lighting Project” in Proposition #10 and added the word “lighting” following the word 
“sidewalks” in Proposition #10, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Opposition was voiced related to the 
removal of the language “17th Street Lighting Project” in Proposition #10 and the determination to 
remove it without obtaining governing body approval.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Segrave voting “no”. (#6157) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the interest and support of the Governing Body in moving 
forward with the ‘Hyatt Place Hotel and Cheyenne Center’ Project.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Comments were made regarding the nature of the proposal and project 
including: city’s capacity to review the new proposal in various aspects of the development, vision 
for the project, public/private partnerships, future projects of this nature, opportunity to learn and 
look at what other municipalities are doing related to similar projects, concern for future limited 
opportunities for development and appreciation was expressed to the proponents for their proposal 
and to members of the governing body for their willingness to consider the project.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#6158) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to accept a grant award from the U.S. 
Department of Treasury for the American Rescue Plan Coronavirus State and Local Fiscal 
Recovery Funds in the amount of $12,191,813.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6159) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events 
Department to apply for a Small Business Administration Shuttered Venue Operators Grant to 
recoup a portion of revenue losses incurred as a result of mandatory coronavirus induced Civic 
Center closures.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6160) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne Community Recreation and Events 
Department to apply for a Cheyenne Community Recreation District Recreation Mill Grant to 
purchase scoreboards and sound systems for the Cheyenne Junior League Athletic Complex.  
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6161) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the expansion of the Capitol North Historic District and the 
addition of one hundred nineteen (119) structures to the contributing structures list maintained by 
the City’s Planning Office.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6162) 
 

   
[CA]

Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Animal Shelter/Services for 
Animal Control and Animal Shelter Services (General Fund).  Mr. Roybal moved to approve in an 
amount not to exceed $425,000, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by 
substitute dated June 30, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. O’Donnell explained the substitute 
noting it is for a period of time not to exceed 6 months and payment is at the monthly rate the 
Cheyenne Animal Shelter requested from the city.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception 
of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#7373) 
 
 
Memorandum of Agreement between Robin and Debbie Courtier, the City of Cheyenne, 
and the Wyoming Department of Transportation for the study of downstream storm water 
problems.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. Roybal reported that 
due to a recent verbal agreement being reached and in an effort to expedite the use of the Detention 
Pond Easement, this item was reviewed under other items for the agenda at the July 6, 2021 
Finance Committee Meeting, therefore, provisions of Title 2 of City  Code have been met.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated July 7, 2021, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. 
O’Donnell reported the substitute includes removal of #10 under the “Now, therefore, in 
consideration of the mutual benefits and burdens assumed by each party to this agreement” 
paragraph relating to costs that will be paid by the City, WYDOT and others.  He advised there 
may be an agreement for these fees in the future that will allow for the cost sharing to be determined 
at a later date.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  Tom Cobb, City Engineer, was complemented 
for his efforts related to the memorandum of agreement and it was noted members of the governing 
body look forward to learning more on the process.  Comments were made that the draining issue 
has had substantial negative effect on how the city evaluates development projects relating to 
drainage concerns.  It was noted that drainage concerns have been expressed by property owners 
further downstream near Child’s Draw.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  
(#7374)

Reappointment of Jay Sullivan to the Laramie County Community Juvenile Services Joint 
Powers Board.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote 
(mayoral appointments).   
 
 
Announcement was made that the July 26, 2021 City Council meeting will be held in City 
Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, and via electronic conference 
meeting at Noon instead of 6:00 p.m. 
 
 
Under other business: 
 
 
Mayor Collins explained he has been working on the Urban Renewal Authority Board and 
has received all applications and would like to appoint the board members at this meeting to allow 
them to address the Hitching Post issue.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, read into the record the names for 
appointment:  Appointment of Katye Brown, Ken Dugas, Hugh Robert Graham, Chet Halvorson, 
and Ryan Whitehead to the Urban Renewal Authority Board.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with 
the exception of Mayor Collins who did not vote (mayoral appointments).   
 
 
Mayor Collins advised he was contacted on Friday, July 9, 2021 by the Fire Union to see 
if they could meet with him and the City Attorney relating to the Fire Department’s contract with 
the city.  Mayor Collins reported he would like to meet in Executive Session regarding the meeting 
held and noted there will be no further action taken after the meeting and that the regular meeting 
of the governing body will be adjourned following adjournment of the Executive Session.  Mr. 
O’Donnell advised there will be no further business after Executive Session and that adjournment 
of the regular meeting will not be on video.  Dr. Rinne moved to meet in executive session at 7:07 
p.m., seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
 
 
Following adjournment of the Executive Session, Mayor Collins reconvened the regular 
meeting of the governing body at 7:33 p.m. and there being no further business to come before the 
governing body, the meeting was officially adjourned at 7:34 p.m.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
